Use when later thread continuation or goal-aware scheduling is needed.\\\"\\nmetadata:\\n  skill-type: team_automation\\n  triggers:\\n    - he heartbeat\\n    - goal-aware scheduling\\n    - until-green checks\\n    - thread continuation\\n---\\n# Harness Engineering Heartbeat\\n## Philosophy\\nContinue only with a clear stop rule. Heartbeats should preserve context, wake the thread with fresh state, and keep scheduling separate from HE lifecycle ownership.\\n## When to Use\\nUse when monitoring, wakeups, until-green loops, or follow-up automation is requested.\\n## Inputs\\nTarget thread/workspace, cadence, stop condition, issue/PR/check links, optional active thread goal.\\n## Outputs\\nReturn schema_version when structured. Heartbeat prompt, status, stop rule, `next_invocation`, `subagent_policy`, slack_policy, blackboard_delta, and next user-visible update.\\n## Procedure\\nPrefer thread heartbeat for this conversation; encode stop criteria; avoid duplicate automations. When `/goal` is active or requested, keep the goal as the persistent objective and the heartbeat as the scheduler with live checks and stop rules.\\n\\nFor GitHub PR sweeps, keep the loop concrete: identify the PR set, re-check GitHub truth, inspect CircleCI/job logs for failing checks, inspect CodeRabbit/Codex review threads, and route each wake-up to the smallest safe `he-code-review` or `he-work` follow-up. Use `git-project-triage` as a helper role only when it is available in the Codex agent manifest; if the role is missing, continue inline and report that delegation gap.\\n## Validation\\nFail fast: stop at the first failed gate and do not proceed. Confirm schedule, destination, and safe prompt scope.\\n## Failure mode\\nIf required evidence, Linear linkage, or next-stage routing is missing, stop and return the blocker with the smallest recovery step.\\n## Constraints\\nRedact secrets; do not create cron workarounds for short thread follow-up. Do not remove important context for budget trimming; move deep context to references.\\n## Anti-Patterns\\n- Creating duplicate wakeups for the same PR, issue, or thread.\\n- Running without an explicit stop condition or next visible update.\\n- Using a heartbeat prompt or `/goal` objective as a replacement for Linear, PR, validation, or lifecycle exit evidence.\\n- Scheduling unattended destructive actions, merges, deploys, or tracker closure without explicit approval.\\n## Examples\\n- \\\"Inspect PR 154 every 30 minutes in this thread until CI is green or a blocker appears.\\\"\\n- \\\"Keep watching JSC-246 after the merge queue starts and wake this thread with the next required action.\\\"\\n- \\\"Rotate across the open coding-harness PRs with GitHub, CircleCI, CodeRabbit, and Codex comments; use `git-project-triage` when available, wake every 30 minutes, and stop only once the PRs are green, merged, or explicitly blocked.\\\"\\n## References\\n- Shared subagent call policy: `Plugins/harness-engineering/references/subagent-call-contract.md`\\n- Deferred context index: `Plugins/harness-engineering/references/deferred-context-index.md`\\n- Goal continuity: `Plugins/harness-engineering/references/goal-continuity.md`\\n- Pragmatic operating invariants: `Plugins/harness-engineering/references/pragmatic-operating-invariants.md`\\n- XP operating contract: `Plugins/harness-engineering/references/xp-operating-contract.md`\\n\",\n  \"encoding\": \"base64\",\n  \"_links\": {\n    \"self\": \"https://api.github.com/repos/jscraik/Agent-Skills/contents/Plugins/harness-engineering/skills/he-heartbeat/SKILL.md?ref=main\",\n    \"git\": \"https://api.github.com/repos/jscraik/Agent-Skills/git/blobs/3c34cf459592485a18ddf0c18534dedcdc8b8cee\",\n    \"html\": \"https://github.com/jscraik/Agent-Skills/blob/main/Plugins/harness-engineering/skills/he-heartbeat/SKILL.md\"\n  }\n}"}],"structured_content":null},"error":null,"status":"completed"}}

Contract\\n\\nUse this at the end of every active HE stage so downstream agents know status, proof, and next step.\\n\\n## Active Spine\\n\\n`he-router -> he-brainstorm -> he-spec -> he-plan -> he-work -> he-code-review -> he-fix-bugs -> he-work|done`\\n\\nUse `he-improve` for measured improvement, `he-compound` for cross-stage orchestration or solved-problem capture, and `he-heartbeat` for wake/re-check loops.\\n\\nFolded stages are modes, not the default route:\\n\\n- `he-ideate` -> `he-brainstorm`\\n- `he-deepen-spec` -> `he-spec`\\n- `he-deepen-plan` -> `he-plan`\\n- `he-tdd` -> `he-work`\\n- `he-refine` -> `he-improve`\\n- `he-technical-review`, `he-reliability-review` -> `he-code-review`\\n- `he-compound-refresh` -> `he-compound`\\n\\n## Required Status Shape\\n\\nWhen true|false|unknown|not_applicable\\n  transition_event: scoped|start|progress_tick|pr_opened|handoff_ready|merged|blocked|unblocked|fail|not_applicable\\n  transition_command: \\\"<harness linear ... harness_commands_run: []\\n  harness_commands_blocked: []\\n```\\n\\nFor short chat responses, summarize the same fields without losing blocker, tracker, artifact, validation, or next-stage state.\\n\\n## Exit Rules\\n\\n- Do not hand off tracked work unless the Linear gate is `resolved`, `created`, `user_opted_out`, or explicitly `blocked`.\\n- Do not route to `he-plan` while behavior, acceptance criteria, or product scope is unresolved.\\n- Do not route to `he-work` while plan/source traceability, validation strategy, or scope boundary is missing.\\n- Do not claim `done` without validation evidence or a concrete reason validation is not applicable.\\n- Do not let a PR, branch, local plan, or session summary replace Linear as tracker of record.\\n- Do not let external review, CI, session, or user wording change HE domain meaning without translation through `domain-context-contract.md`.\\n- In coding-harness-managed repos, lifecycle transitions require populated or explicitly blocked Harness command bridge fields.\\n\\n## Stage Minimums\\n\\n- `he-router`: selected stage, confidence, matched rule, missing input if blocked.\\n- `he-brainstorm`: problem frame, scope tier, spec decision, Linear tracker state for durable handoff, next stage.\\n- `he-spec`: accepted behavior contract, stable acceptance IDs, Linear Work Item Contract, next planning slice.\\n- `he-plan`: stable units, dependencies, tests, rollback, Linear/spec/plan/PR traceability matrix.\\n- `he-work`: changed slices, completed IDs, validation evidence, drift notes, PR/Linear handoff.\\n- `he-code-review`: findings, readiness verdict, evidence ladder, next action.\\n- `he-fix-bugs`: reproduction, root cause, fix/verification status, regression test recommendation.\\n- `he-improve`: baseline, measured delta, accepted/rejected experiment, rollback posture.\\n- `he-compound`: mode, earliest incomplete stage, stage exit evidence, next exact stage.\\n- `he-heartbeat`: live checks, cadence, stop conditions, next stage when the heartbeat wakes.\\n\\n## Coding Harness Managed Repos\\n\\nWhen `harness.contract.json`, `.harness/`, or Harness gates are present, load `references/coding-harness-command-bridge.md`. Record Project Brain and north-star evidence using the exact bridge vocabularies: `project_brain_status` is `updated|blocked|not_checked|not_applicable`, and `north_star_evidence_status` is `pass|blocked|not_checked|not_applicable`. A blocked or not-checked status must preserve the exact missing command/path in `harness_commands_blocked` or the matching evidence field.\\n\\n## Cut Policy\\n\\nPrefer one active stage plus one folded mode over a niche stage. Add a first-class stage only for distinct inputs, exit evidence, and a clear routing boundary.\\n\",\n  \"encoding\": \"base64\",\n  \"_links\": {\n    \"self\": \"https://api.github.com/repos/jscraik/Agent-Skills/contents/Plugins/harness-engineering/references/lifecycle-exit-contract.md?ref=main\",\n    \"git\": \"https://api.github.com/repos/jscraik/Agent-Skills/git/blobs/dbeed1a18c2330854fec9f63be59c212558a85cc\",\n    \"html\": \"https://github.com/jscraik/Agent-Skills/blob/main/Plugins/harness-engineering/references/lifecycle-exit-contract.md\"\n  }\n}"}],"structured_content":null},"error":null,"status":"completed"}}

to make a routed skill mentionable, for example `$he-heartbeat`; the real workflow remains in the resolved **Canonical Skill Source**. {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_29","type":"mcp_tool_call","server":"codex_apps","tool":"github_fetch_file","arguments":{"repository_full_name":"jscraik/Agent-Skills","path":"Plugins/harness-engineering/references/xp-operating-contract.md","ref":"main","encoding":"utf-8"},"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"{\"content\": \"# XP Operating Contract\\n\\nHarness Engineering favors simple, evidence-backed improvement loops. This\\ncontract keeps staged work close to the failing behavior and prevents ceremony\\nfrom replacing working software.\\n\\n## Simple Design\\n\\n- Make the current behavior observable before changing it.\\n- For repo operations, use `./bin/ask` as the default command surface; it forwards to `Infrastructure/bin/ask`.\\n- If an operation is unavailable via `./bin/ask`, state the exception and rationale.\\n- Prefer a narrow test or eval that would have caught the regression.\\n- Keep names and states consistent across native tools, skill output, and\\n  generated evidence.\\n\\n## Red Signals\\n\\n- A fix cannot name the exact failing command, comment, or artifact.\\n- A stage adds process without reducing future decision load.\\n- A handoff hides an unresolved blocker behind generic \\\"follow-up\\\" wording.\\n- A workflow asks agents to infer state that can be read from a command or file.\\n\\n## Slack Policy\\n\\nUse `slack_policy` to state intentional tolerance:\\n\\n```yaml\\nslack_policy:\\n  status: none|bounded|blocked\\n  reason: \\\"<why slack exists>\\\"\\n  limit: \\\"<time, token, scope, or risk boundary>\\\"\\n  recovery: \\\"<how to tighten it later>\\\"\\n```\\n\\nBounded slack is acceptable for exploratory stages. It is not acceptable for\\nmerge readiness, security findings, failing CI, or unresolved review threads.\\n\\n## Learning Loop\\n\\nWhen the same issue recurs, fix the active issue first, then update one durable\\nsurface so the route improves next time. Good destinations are skill wording,\\ncontract examples, eval fixtures, validation wrappers, and high-traffic docs.\\n\", \"encoding\": \"utf-8\", \"sha\": \"558e84d65978fbc9d6364ea60d3642d653fd857b\", \"display_url\": \"https://github.com/jscraik/Agent-Skills/blob/main/Plugins/harness-engineering/references/xp-operating-contract.md\", \"display_title\": \"xp-operating-contract.md\"}"}],"structured_content":null},"error":null,"status":"completed"}}

{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_30","type":"mcp_tool_call","server":"codex_apps","tool":"github_fetch_file","arguments":{"repository_full_name":"jscraik/Agent-Skills","path":"Plugins/harness-engineering/skills/goal-governor/SKILL.md","ref":"main","encoding":"utf-8"},"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"{\"content\": \"---\\nname: goal-governor\\ndescription: Create, continue, and audit Codex persistent-goal work with repo-visible goal boards, native goal reconciliation, scoped agent tasks, receipts, and verification freshness gates. Use when a user wants durable `/goal` workflows, long-running Codex goal governance, or safe continuation of stalled goal work.\\nmetadata:\\n  skill-type: team_automation\\n  triggers:\\n    - goal governor\\n    - /goal workflows\\n    - goal workflows\\n    - durable goal governance\\n  lifecycle_state: active\\n  maturity: experimental\\n  owner: Harness Engineering Team\\n  review_cadence: quarterly\\n  metadata_source: frontmatter\\n  quality_target: plugin-eval-a\\n---\\n\\n# Goal Governor\\n\\n## Philosophy\\n\\nUse this skill to turn Codex's native `/goal` feature into an auditable operating loop. Native goals persist a thread objective and runtime status; this skill governs the repo-visible plan, active task, receipts, and completion audit.\\n\\nTreat goal work as a state machine. Know the native status, board status, active task, write scope, and required evidence before continuing.\\n\\n## When To Use\\n\\nUse when the user asks to create, continue, repair, audit, or operationalize a long-running Codex goal, especially when work is broad, stale, blocked, multi-agent, high-risk, or likely to span sessions.\\n\\nDo not use for ordinary one-file fixes, quick questions, or implementation tasks where the user has not asked for durable `/goal` governance.\\n\\n## Modes\\n\\n- `create`: scaffold a goal board, verify native goal support, and print `/goal Follow <goal.md>`.\\n- `continue`: read native goal state and the board, reconcile drift, and select the next safe action before implementation.\\n- `doctor`: check installation, Codex goal enablement, objective fit, agent runtime depth, board validity, and verification freshness.\\n- `check`: validate a goal board and receipts without changing files.\\n- `repair`: propose or apply low-risk board repairs only after reporting the exact drift.\\n- `import`: convert an existing spec, plan, or issue into a goal board without losing its original source path.\\n\\n## Required Inputs\\n\\n- Target repository path.\\n- Goal intent, existing goal path, or artifact to import.\\n- Editable boundary for any write-capable task.\\n- Verification command contract from the target repo.\\n- Stop condition, such as owner input, red validation, scope expansion, or completion audit.\\n\\n## Outputs\\n\\n- Board health report with native/board reconciliation.\\n- Created or repaired board files when the mode allows edits.\\n- Next safe action classification.\\n- Machine-checkable validation evidence.\\n- `blackboard_delta` and `slack_policy` for long-running continuation.\\n- Residual risks or owner-input blockers.\\n\\n## Workflow\\n\\n1. Read the nearest project instructions first. In `~/dev/codex`, read `instructions/CODESTYLE.md` when present before technical edits.\\n2. Run `doctor` checks before `create` or `continue`:\\n   - `[features].goals = true` is configured or the user accepts a blocked setup.\\n   - Native objective is non-empty and no more than 4,000 characters; keep bulk instructions in `goal.md`.\\n   - Agent config supports the intended delegation depth; for Jamie's Codex harness, require `max_depth >= 2`.\\n   - The selected validation commands exist and are repo-canonical.\\n   - Existing board files pass `python3 scripts/check_goal_board.py <goal-directory>`.\\n3. Reconcile native and board state, including `active`, `paused`, `budgetLimited`, and `complete` (normalize native `budgetLimited` to output `budget_limited`).\\n4. Treat token budget, tokens used, elapsed seconds, lifecycle updates, and budget-limited transitions as evidence, not completion proof.\\n5. Ensure exactly one active task unless the user explicitly requested parallel Workers with disjoint `allowed_files`.\\n6. Refuse write-capable work until the active Worker task declares `allowed_files`, `verify`, and `stop_if`.\\n7. If verification is missing, red, stale, blocked, or from a different dirty fingerprint, recover verification before feature work.\\n8. After each task, append a machine-checkable receipt and select the next safe task.\\n9. Mark a goal complete only after a final Judge or PM audit receipt with `decision: complete`; then update the native goal status.\\n\\n## Goal Directory\\n\\nDefault layout:\\n\\n```text\\ndocs/goals/<slug>/\\n  goal.md\\n  state.yaml\\n  receipts.jsonl\\n  notes/\\n```\\n\\n`state.yaml` owns current state. `goal.md` explains intent. `receipts.jsonl` is append-only evidence. `notes/` is for bulky supporting material only.\\n\\n## Safety Rules\\n\\n- `/goal Follow docs/goals/<slug>/goal.md` is a prompt convention, not a native file binding. Always read `goal.md` and `state.yaml` before acting.\\n- Native `/goal` objective text is validated by Codex. Long plans belong in repo files.\\n- Redact secrets, credentials, tokens, private keys, and sensitive personal data by default in receipts, notes, examples, and chat output.\\n- Do not edit implementation files until board health, native-goal reconciliation, and verification freshness are known.\\n- Do not write outside `allowed_files` for an active Worker task.\\n- Do not treat generated templates as proof that agents are installed; verify runtime config and role availability.\\n- Do not install or mutate `~/.codex` directly unless the user asks for a direct runtime install. Prefer canonical repo projection paths.\\n- If native goal state and board state conflict, stop implementation and classify the mismatch.\\n- Do not remove important context for budget trimming; move deep context to references instead of deleting it.\\n\\n## Anti-Patterns\\n\\n- Treating `/goal Follow <path>` as a native file binding instead of a prompt convention.\\n- Starting Worker implementation before reading `goal.md`, `state.yaml`, and recent receipts.\\n- Marking a goal complete without a Judge or PM completion receipt.\\n- Broadening Worker scope silently when files outside `allowed_files` are needed.\\n- Installing directly into `~/.codex` when the project has a canonical projection layer.\\n- Continuing from conversation memory when board state or verification evidence is stale.\\n\\n## Gotchas\\n\\n- Native `/goal` state and repo-visible board state can drift. Reconcile both before choosing Worker work.\\n- A receipt is only useful when it names the exact verifier and outcome; vague \\\"tested\\\" notes are not completion evidence.\\n- `budgetLimited` is a native stop/steering state that maps to normalized output status `budget_limited`. Classify scope, verification, and owner decision before Worker work.\\n- Delegation still follows the shared HE subagent call contract; do not assume Scout, Judge, or Worker roles exist until runtime config proves they are available.\\n\\n## Output Contract\\n\\nReturn:\\n\\n```yaml\\nschema_version: 1\\nmode: create|continue|doctor|check|repair|import\\ngoal_path: path-or-null\\nnative_goal_status: active|paused|budget_limited|complete|missing|unknown|blocked\\nboard_status: active|paused|done|blocked|invalid|missing\\nnext_action: continue|scout|judge|worker|repair|ask_owner|stop\\nvalidation_evidence:\\n  - command: exact command\\n    outcome: pass|fail|blocked\\n    note: short evidence\\nrisks:\\n  - short residual risk\\n```\\n\\n## Progressive Disclosure\\n\\n- Read [references/deferred-context-index.md](../../references/deferred-context-index.md) when preserving or recovering context moved out of active skill text.\\n- Read [references/subagent-call-contract.md](../../references/subagent-call-contract.md) before delegating Scout, Judge, PM, or Worker tasks.\\n- Read [references/native-goal-runtime.md](./references/native-goal-runtime.md) when reconciling with current `~/dev/codex` native goal behavior.\\n- Read [references/goal-contract.md](./references/goal-contract.md) when creating or validating `goal.md`, `state.yaml`, or `receipts.jsonl`.\\n- Read [references/creation-and-continuation.md](./references/creation-and-continuation.md) when choosing create, continue, repair, or import behavior.\\n- Read [references/evals.yaml](./references/evals.yaml) when testing this skill with binary trigger and behavior checks.\\n- Read [../../references/pragmatic-operating-invariants.md](../../references/pragmatic-operating-invariants.md) when continuation exposes repeated drift, stale state, or broken-window cleanup.\\n- Read [../../references/xp-operating-contract.md](../../references/xp-operating-contract.md) when emitting blackboard deltas, slack policy, red signals, or repeated-failure learning.\\n\\n## Validation\\n\\nValidate the skill package:\\n\\n```bash\\npython3 scripts/check_goal_board.py <goal-directory>\\n./bin/ask skills audit <skill-directory> --level strict --robot\\n```\\n\\nFail fast: stop at the first failed gate, fix the blocker, and rerun the exact failed command before proceeding.\\n\\nWhen changing this skill, use an Autoresearch-style loop: baseline first, patch one hypothesis, run the smallest verifier and guard, then keep or discard with evidence.\\n\\n## Examples\\n\\n- \\\"Create a `/goal` board for `JSC-190` in `coding-harness`; tomorrow's run must start with `bash scripts/run-harness-setup-checks.sh`.\\\"\\n- \\\"Continue `docs/goals/codex-goal-governance/goal.md`; I moved from `main` to `feature/jscraik-agent-first-golden-path-spec-plan`, so verify before editing.\\\"\\n- \\\"Doctor the overnight Codex config cleanup goal before it runs; check native goals, worker scope, and Scout/Judge/Worker availability.\\\"\\n\", \"encoding\": \"utf-8\", Use these invariants when repeated review comments, route confusion,\\nstale evidence, or skill bloat appear across HE stages.\\n\\n## Invariants\\n\\n- Prefer the smallest durable repair surface that prevents recurrence.\\n- Preserve source-of-truth ownership; do not patch generated projections as the\\n  canonical fix.\\n- Convert repeated feedback into one of: skill instruction, eval fixture,\\n  validation contract, reference note, or explicit rejection.\\n- Keep evidence current. Stale session, PR, CI, or goal evidence must be\\n  refreshed or labeled as stale before it drives decisions.\\n- Avoid broad skill expansion when one precise contract or command wrapper would\\n  remove the failure mode.\\n\\n## Design Complexity Red Flags\\n\\n- A skill needs another skill to explain its basic output.\\n- The same blocker appears in two consecutive PRs without a captured learning.\\n- A generated artifact is treated as proof even though its status is failing,\\n  stale, or diagnostic-only.\\n- Multiple names describe the same workflow state without an explicit mapping.\\n- Operators are routed to raw scripts when a repo wrapper exists.\\n\\n## Blackboard Delta\\n\\nWhen a stage learns something durable, emit a compact `blackboard_delta`:\\n\\n```yaml\\nblackboard_delta:\\n  status: added|updated|not_applicable\\n  learning: \\\"<one sentence>\\\"\\n  target_surface: skill|reference|eval|validator|plan|memory|not_applicable\\n  owner: \\\"<skill or workflow owner>\\\"\\n  follow_up: \\\"<smallest next action or null>\\\"\\n```\\n\\nUse `not_applicable` when the run only applies an existing contract and creates\\nno new reusable learning.\\n\", \"encoding\": \"utf-8\", \"sha\": \"ed3de9caa63998a3c22cdba447744e5ea652ea36\", \"display_url\": \"https://github.com/jscraik/Agent-Skills/blob/main/Plugins/harness-engineering/references/pragmatic-operating-invariants.md\", \"display_title\": \"pragmatic-operating-invariants.md\"}"}],"structured_content":null},"error":null,"status":"completed"}}

{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_38","type":"mcp_tool_call","server":"codex_apps","tool":"github_fetch_file","arguments":{"repository_full_name":"jscraik/Agent-Skills","path":"Plugins/harness-engineering/references/domain-context-contract.md","ref":"main","encoding":"utf-8"},"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"{\"content\": \"# Domain Context Contract\\n\\nRead when: Harness Engineering work depends on project terminology, cross-artifact meaning, external review/log input, or a handoff between Linear, specs, plans, PRs, validation, Project Brain, heartbeats, goals, or session evidence.\\n\\nThis contract translates domain-driven design practices into agent-native HE checks. Use it to preserve meaning without expanding every active `SKILL.md`.\\n\\n## Core Rule\\n\\nHE stages preserve intent by translating between bounded contexts, not by copying wording blindly. A stage that changes or consumes domain meaning must name the source context, translation, conflict status, and next owner before handing off.\\n\\n## Domain Language Envelope\\n\\nUse this envelope when terminology affects behavior, acceptance criteria, tracker wording, code names, review findings, or user expectations:\\n\\n```yaml\\ndomain_language:\\n  status: stable|ambiguous|conflicted|not_applicable\\n  canonical_terms: []\\n  avoided_aliases: []\\n  unresolved_terms: []\\n  context_file: CONTEXT.md|CONTEXT-MAP.md|not_applicable\\n```\\n\\nStage guidance:\\n\\n- `he-brainstorm`: surface fuzzy terms, avoided aliases, and the question that will settle meaning.\\n- `he-spec`: lock canonical terms into behavior, acceptance IDs, and non-goals.\\n- `he-plan`: preserve canonical terms in plan units without renumbering source IDs.\\n- `he-work`: stop when implementation reveals term drift and update the owning artifact before coding past it.\\n- `he-code-review`: flag code, docs, spec, plan, or Linear term drift before readiness synthesis.\\n- `he-compound`: reconcile stale or conflicting lifecycle artifacts before selecting the next stage.\\n\\n## Context Map Envelope\\n\\nUse this compact context map when HE state moves between artifacts:\\n\\n```yaml\\ncontext_map:\\n  source_of_truth: user_request|linear|spec|plan|worktree|pr|validation|project_brain|heartbeat|goal|session_evidence|not_applicable\\n  translated_from: []\\n  relationship: source_of_truth|translation|handoff|evidence_only|stale_snapshot|not_applicable\\n  conflict_status: none|blocked|resolved|not_applicable\\n  conflict_rule: stop|refresh|prefer_source|record_blocker|not_applicable\\n```\\n\\nDefault conflict rules:\\n\\n- Prefer the freshest owning artifact for the current stage.\\n- Treat session memory and old summaries as evidence labels until refreshed.\\n- Stop when Linear, spec, plan, or PR names disagree about behavior and the disagreement changes acceptance or implementation.\\n- Record the smallest refresh command, artifact path, or user question when a context cannot be reconciled.\\n\\n## Anti-Corruption Translation\\n\\nExternal or neighboring models must be translated before they change HE artifacts:\\n\\n| Input model | Translate into | Do not treat as |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| User shorthand | canonical HE task, stage, and missing evidence | direct implementation permission |\\n| Linear issue wording | tracker contract and acceptance source | complete spec by itself |\\n| CodeRabbit/Codex/human review | finding, disproven signal, deferred item, or HE follow-up | unquestioned blocker |\\n| CI or test log | reproduced failure, blocker, or validation evidence | root cause without reproduction |\\n| Session evidence | bounded evidence label with freshness | current repository truth |\\n| External source material | invariant, eval, contract field, reference, or explicit rejection | pasted doctrine |\\n\\n## Core Domain\\n\\nThe HE core domain is preserving intent, evidence, and lifecycle truth across agent work. Protect these concepts first:\\n\\n- stage boundary\\n- source traceability\\n- evidence freshness\\n- acceptance IDs\\n- validation evidence\\n- tracker state\\n- blackboard delta\\n- blocked exit\\n- next stage\\n- domain language and term drift\\n\\nSupporting docs, examples, reviewer fanout, and implementation sketches are secondary. Move them to references or artifacts when context pressure rises.\\n\\n## Completion Check\\n\\nBefore a domain-sensitive HE handoff, verify:\\n\\n- canonical terms and avoided aliases are recorded or marked not applicable;\\n- source context and target context are explicit when artifacts disagree;\\n- conflict resolution is `resolved`, `blocked`, or `not_applicable`;\\n- external inputs have been translated through the anti-corruption table;\\n- the next stage knows the owning artifact and smallest refresh step.\\n\", \"encoding\": \"utf-8\", \"sha\": \"139188247e20310da289ad9aea7cba18f4fb521e\", \"display_url\": \"https://github.com/jscraik/Agent-Skills/blob/main/Plugins/harness-engineering/references/domain-context-contract.md\", \"display_title\": \"domain-context-contract.md\"}"}],"structured_content":null},"error":null,"status":"completed"}}

{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_39","type":"mcp_tool_call","server":"codex_apps","tool":"github_fetch_file","arguments":{"repository_full_name":"jscraik/Agent-Skills","path":"Plugins/harness-engineering/references/design-complexity-contract.md","ref":"main","encoding":"utf-8"},"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"{\"content\": \"# Design Complexity Contract\\n\\nRead when: improving, reviewing, or routing Harness Engineering skills where a change may alter stage boundaries, context handoffs, output contracts, or caller cognitive load.\\n\\nThis contract keeps HE stages deep, small, and agent-native. It complements `domain-context-contract.md`: domain context protects meaning; design complexity protects the interface that carries that meaning.\\n\\n## Interface Goal\\n\\nEach HE stage should be a deep module: a small caller-facing contract with enough behavior hidden behind it that the next agent can act without carrying unrelated lifecycle state in working memory.\\n\\nPrefer changing shared contracts, evals, or references when the same rule would otherwise need to be copied across multiple stage entrypoints.\\n\\n## Complexity Red Flags\\n\\nClassify at least one red flag before changing HE skill behavior:\\n\\n- `change_amplification`: one behavior change requires edits to several unrelated stage files.\\n- `cognitive_load`: the caller must know stage internals, validation policy, tracker rules, or output shapes that the stage could own.\\n- `unknown_unknown`: the skill does not say which evidence must be fresh before action.\\n- `obscurity`: rule ownership is unclear between `SKILL.md`, `contract.yaml`, `evals.yaml`, and references.\\n- `shallow_stage`: a stage mostly passes through to another stage without adding a distinct abstraction.\\n- `leaked_state`: Linear, PR, Project Brain, validation, heartbeat, or goal state must be repeated manually instead of moving through a lifecycle exit.\\n- `special_case_sprawl`: a narrow prompt fix adds another branch where a general stage contract or eval would reduce future branches.\\n- `term_drift`: Linear, spec, plan, code, or review names diverge in ways that can change behavior.\\n- `context_bleed`: one HE stage or artifact silently applies another context's model without translation.\\n- `source_model_corruption`: external review, CI, session, or user wording changes HE meaning without anti-corruption translation.\\n- `unmapped_context_handoff`: an artifact handoff lacks source context, target context, freshness, or conflict status.\\n- `core_domain_obscured`: supporting workflow detail hides HE's core domain of intent, evidence, and lifecycle truth.\\n\\n## Shared Output Envelope\\n\\nWhen a stage returns structured output or hands off to another HE stage, include the lifecycle exit contract fields and preserve this compact envelope:\\n\\n```yaml\\nschema_version: 1\\nhe_stage: \\\"<he-router|he-brainstorm|he-spec|he-plan|he-work|he-code-review|he-fix-bugs|he-improve|he-compound|he-heartbeat>\\\"\\nstatus: pass|blocked|needs_route|needs_user\\nowned_artifact: \\\"<path or not_applicable>\\\"\\nevidence_freshness: fresh|stale|blocked|not_applicable\\nred_flags: []\\ndomain_language:\\n  status: stable|ambiguous|conflicted|not_applicable\\n  canonical_terms: []\\n  avoided_aliases: []\\n  unresolved_terms: []\\n  context_file: CONTEXT.md|CONTEXT-MAP.md|domain-context-contract.md|not_applicable\\ncontext_map:\\n  source_of_truth: user_request|linear|spec|plan|worktree|pr|validation|project_brain|heartbeat|goal|session_evidence|not_applicable\\n  translated_from: []\\n  relationship: source_of_truth|translation|handoff|evidence_only|stale_snapshot|not_applicable\\n  conflict_status: none|blocked|resolved|not_applicable\\n  conflict_rule: stop|refresh|prefer_source|record_blocker|not_applicable\\nblocker: \\\"<smallest recovery step or null>\\\"\\nnext_stage: he-router|he-brainstorm|he-spec|he-plan|he-work|he-code-review|he-fix-bugs|he-improve|he-compound|he-heartbeat|done\\nvalidation:\\n  status: pass|fail|blocked|not_run_with_reason|not_applicable\\n  commands: []\\n  outcomes: []\\n```\\n\\nStage-specific output may add fields, but it should not omit blocker, evidence freshness, validation status, or next-stage state when those are relevant.\\n\\n## Design It Twice\\n\\nBefore changing a stage interface, sketch two alternatives:\\n\\n- `patch_design`: the smallest local edit to the current stage.\\n- `interface_design`: a shared contract, reference, or eval change that reduces future caller burden.\\n\\nChoose by answering:\\n\\n- Which design makes the caller know less?\\n- Which design changes fewer files next time?\\n- Which design has the clearer eval?\\n\\nIf the local patch wins, record why the shared interface would be overreach. If the shared interface wins, keep `SKILL.md` to a signpost plus the stage-specific rule.\\n\\n## Ownership Rule\\n\\nKeep each rule in the narrowest durable owner:\\n\\n- `routing-map.json`: stage selection rules.\\n- `SKILL.md`: stage boundary, caller contract, and signposts.\\n- `contract.yaml`: inputs, outputs, risks, observability, rollback.\\n- `evals.yaml`: executable or reviewable proof cases.\\n- `references/*.md`: rationale, retained context, cross-stage contracts.\\n\\nWhen the same rule appears in more than one owner, keep the canonical owner and replace duplicates with a signpost unless the duplicate is required for runtime routing.\\n\\n## Evaluation Requirements\\n\\nAdd or update evals when a change addresses any of these failure modes:\\n\\n- repeated feedback that should become a broken-window classification;\\n- low-confidence router output that needs deterministic clarification;\\n- pass-through stage behavior that should be folded or split;\\n- stale evidence acted on as fresh;\\n- duplicated ownership rules with conflicting wording;\\n- an improvement made without recording the red flag or design alternative;\\n- domain term drift, context bleed, or source-model corruption changes behavior without a mapped handoff.\\n\\n## Stop Rule\\n\\nA design-complexity improvement is not complete until:\\n\\n- the changed skill still passes strict audit;\\n- at least one eval or contract field covers the triggering red flag;\\n- retained rationale is in a reference, not bloated entrypoint prose;\\n- lifecycle exit output still includes blocker, freshness, validation, and next-stage state;\\n- remaining limitations are explicit blockers or non-goals.\\n\", \"encoding\": \"utf-8\", \"sha\": \"460adcbb4183d733d9c60a6bf4916553421bc582\", \"display_url\": \"https://github.com/jscraik/Agent-Skills/blob/main/Plugins/harness-engineering/references/design-complexity-contract.md\", \"display_title\":
